r\coloneqq(\ad^{\mathfrak{g}})^{*}(\ad^{\mathfrak{gl}(\mathfrak{g})})
and so its differential
\delta_{\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}}
is given by

\displaystyle\delta_{\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}}(\omega)(x_{1},\dots,x_{k+1})(y)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{k+1}(-1)^{i+1}\left[x_{i},\omega(x_{1},\dots,\widehat{x_{i}},\dots,x_{k+1})(y)\right]

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{k+1}(-1)^{i}\omega\left(x_{1},\dots,\widehat{x_{i}},\dots,x_{k+1}\right)([x_{i},y])

\displaystyle+\sum_{i<j}(-1)^{i+j}\omega\left([x_{i},x_{j}],x_{1},\dots,\widehat{x_{i}},\dots,\widehat{x_{j}},\dots,x_{k+1}\right)(y)

for
\omega\in C^{k}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{gl}(\mathfrak{g}))
and
x_{1},\dots,x_{k+1},y\in\mathfrak{g}
.
This differential restricts to the subspace
C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{gl}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{i}))
of
C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{gl}(\mathfrak{g}))
and
(i_{\mathfrak{gl}(\mathfrak{g})})_{*}\colon\big(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{gl}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{i})),\delta_{\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}}\big)\hookrightarrow\big(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{gl}(\mathfrak{g})),\delta_{\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}}\big)
is a cochain map.

The category of cochain complexes is an abelian category and so kernels and cokernels exist in it. If
\Phi\colon(B^{\bullet},d_{B})\to(A^{\bullet},d_{A})
is a cochain map, its kernel
\Ker^{\bullet}(\Phi)
, its image
\img^{\bullet}(\Phi)
and its cokernel
\Coker^{\bullet}(\Phi)\coloneqq B^{\bullet}/\img^{\bullet}(\Phi)
complexes are defined degree-wise. Denote by
p_{\Coker}\colon(A^{\bullet},d_{A})\twoheadrightarrow(\Coker^{\bullet}(\Phi),\overline{d_{A}})
, the canonical projection, where
\overline{d_{A}}
is exactly defined such that
p_{\Coker}
is a cochain map:
\overline{d_{A}}\circ p_{\Coker}=p_{\Coker}\circ d_{A}
.

Proposition 4.4
.

The deformation complex
C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
of an ideal
\mathfrak{i}\lhd\mathfrak{g}
fits into the following short exact sequence of cochain complexes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{gl}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{i})),\delta_{\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{gl}(\mathfrak{g})),\delta_{\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}),\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}\big).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i_{\mathfrak{gl}({\mathfrak{g}})})_{*}}

Proof.

It is sufficient to check that the cokernel complex of the inclusion
(i_{\mathfrak{gl}({\mathfrak{g}})})_{*}
is isomorphic to the deformation complex
C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
. Consider, for any
k\geq 0,
the surjective linear map

\pi_{*}\colon C^{k}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{gl}(\mathfrak{g}))\to C^{k}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}),\quad\pi_{*}(\omega)(x_{1},\dots,x_{k})=\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ\omega(x_{1},\dots,x_{k})|_{\mathfrak{i}}.

The following direct computation shows that
\pi_{*}
is a cochain map:

\displaystyle\pi_{*}\big(\delta_{\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}}(\omega)\big)(x_{1},\dots,x_{k+1})(u)

\displaystyle=\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\big(\delta_{\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}}(\omega)(x_{1},\dots,x_{k+1})(u)\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{k+1}(-1)^{i+1}\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\big([x_{i},\omega(x_{1},\dots,\widehat{x_{i}},\dots,x_{k+1})(u)]\big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{k+1}(-1)^{i}\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\big(\omega(x_{1},\dots,\widehat{x_{i}},\dots,x_{k+1})([x_{i},u])\big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i<j}(-1)^{i+j}\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\big(\omega([x_{i},x_{j}],x_{1},\dots,\widehat{x_{i}},\dots,\widehat{x_{j}},\dots,x_{k+1})(u)\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{k+1}(-1)^{i+1}\ad^{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}_{x_{i}}\big(\pi_{*}(\omega)(x_{1},\dots,\widehat{x_{i}},\dots,x_{k+1})(u)\big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i<j}(-1)^{i+j}\pi_{*}(\omega)\big([x_{i},x_{j}],x_{1},\dots,\widehat{x_{i}},\dots,\widehat{x_{j}},\dots,x_{k+1}\big)(u)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{k+1}(-1)^{i}\pi_{*}(\omega)\big(x_{1},\dots,\widehat{x_{i}},\dots,x_{k+1}\big)([x_{i},u])

\displaystyle=\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}\big(\pi_{*}(\omega)\big)(x_{1},\dots,x_{k+1},u)

for
x_{1},\ldots,x_{k+1}\in\mathfrak{g}
and
u\in\mathfrak{i}
.
The kernel complex of
\pi_{*}
is exactly the complex
C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{gl}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{i}))
. The following commutative diagram is then automatically a commutative diagram of cochain complexes, that yields the desired isomorphism.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{gl}(\mathfrak{g})),\delta_{\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}),\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigg(\frac{C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{gl}(\mathfrak{g}))}{C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{gl}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{i}))},\overline{\delta_{\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}}}\bigg)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\Coker}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

∎

4.2.2.
Relation with the deformation cohomology of the canonical projection
\mathfrak{g}\to\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}

The ideal
\mathfrak{i}
of
\mathfrak{g}
defines the quotient Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}
and the canonical projection
\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\colon\mathfrak{g}\to\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}
, which is an epimorphism of Lie algebras.

Recall here that the shift by
k
of a cochain complex
(A^{\bullet},d_{A})
is
(A^{\bullet}[k],(-)^{k}d_{A})
, by convention. In this section, several cochain complexes are shifted by
1
, giving a minus sign to the ‘shifted’ differentials.
Note that whenever a cochain complex
(A^{\bullet}=\oplus_{k\geq 0}A^{k}[-k],d_{A})
is shifted by 1 in this section, the shift by 1 is understood to be truncated to non-negative degrees; i.e.
A^{\bullet}[1]
is understood to be
\oplus_{k\geq 1}A^{k}[-k+1]
.

Proposition 4.5
.

There is a natural cochain map from the deformation complex of the canonical projection
\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\colon\mathfrak{g}\to\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}
, as a Lie algebra morphism, to the deformation complex of the ideal
\mathfrak{i}\lhd\mathfrak{g}
, defined by:

\Pi\colon(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})[1],-\delta_{\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}})\to(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}),\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom})

C^{k+1}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})\ni\phi\mapsto(-1)^{k+1}\phi\arrowvert_{(\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{g})\wedge\mathfrak{i}}

for all
k\geq 0
,
which in addition sends deformation cocycles to deformation cocycles.

Denote the image complex of
\Pi
by
C_{\wedge}^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})\coloneqq\{\phi|_{\wedge^{\bullet}\mathfrak{g}\wedge\mathfrak{i}}\ |\ \phi\in\wedge^{\bullet+1}\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}\}
and the restriction of
\Pi
to its image by
\Pi_{\wedge}
. Note that there is no better description of this complex – it needs to be defined as the image of the map
\Pi
.

Proof.

Choose
\phi\in\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}
. Then the following computation shows that
\Pi\circ(-\delta_{\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}})(\phi)=\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}\circ\Pi(\phi)
and so
\Pi
is a cochain map.
Compute for
x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}\in\mathfrak{g}
and
x_{k+1}\in\mathfrak{i}

\displaystyle(-1)^{k+1}(-\delta_{\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/{\mathfrak{i}}}}(\phi))|_{\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{g}\wedge\mathfrak{i}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{k},\underbrace{x_{k+1}}_{\in\mathfrak{i}})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{k}\sum_{i=1}^{k+1}(-1)^{i+1}(\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}^{*}\overline{\ad})_{x_{i}}(\phi(x_{1},\dots,\widehat{x_{i}},\dots,x_{k+1}))

\displaystyle\quad+(-1)^{k}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq k+1}(-1)^{i+j}\phi([x_{i},x_{j}],x_{1},\dots,\widehat{x_{i}},\dots,\widehat{x_{j}},\dots,x_{k},,x_{k+1})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{k}\sum_{i=1}^{k}(-1)^{i+1}\ad^{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}_{x_{i}}(\phi(x_{1},\dots,\widehat{x_{i}},\dots,x_{k},x_{k+1}))

\displaystyle\quad+(-1)^{k}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq k}(-1)^{i+j}\phi([x_{i},x_{j}],x_{1},\dots,\widehat{x_{i}},\dots,\widehat{x_{j}},\dots,x_{k},x_{k+1})

\displaystyle\quad+(-1)^{k}\sum_{i=1}^{k}(-1)^{i}\phi(x_{1},\dots,\widehat{x_{i}},\dots,x_{k},[x_{i},x_{k+1}])

\displaystyle=(-1)^{k}\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}(\phi|_{\wedge^{k-1}\mathfrak{g}\wedge\mathfrak{i}})(x_{1},\dots,x_{k},x_{k+1})

\displaystyle=\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}((-1)^{k}\phi|_{\wedge^{k-1}\mathfrak{g}\wedge\mathfrak{i}})(x_{1},\dots,x_{k},x_{k+1}),

where the second equality uses that
\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}^{*}\overline{\ad}=\ad^{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}
and
\ad^{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}_{x_{k+1}}=0
.
For the second claim, let
\widetilde{\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}}\colon I\times\mathfrak{g}\to\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}
with
\widetilde{\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}}(t,\cdot)\eqqcolon\pi^{t}_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}
be a smooth deformation of the canonical projection
\pi^{0}_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\coloneqq\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}
.
Note that the dimension of
\Ker(\pi_{t})
can possibly vary but since the surjectivity of
\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}
is an open condition, at least for
t
in a sufficiently small neighborhood
J\subset I
of
0
, the rank of
\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}^{t}
remains maximal. Assume hence without loss of generality that the deformation of
\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}
is deformed by surjective Lie algebra morphisms.
The induced deformation of
\mathfrak{i}\coloneqq\Ker(\mathfrak{\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}})
as an ideal is given by
(\mathfrak{i}_{t}:=\Ker(\pi^{t}_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}))_{t\in I}
and the smoothness of this curve in the Grassmannian is guaranteed by the smoothness of
\widetilde{\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}}
.

Pick a smooth curve of linear vector space isomorphisms
(\alpha_{t})_{t\in J\subset I}
in
\GL(\mathfrak{g})
with
\alpha_{0}=\Id
and
\alpha_{t}(\mathfrak{i})=\mathfrak{i}_{t}
for all
t\in J
and consider the linear map
\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}^{t}\circ\alpha_{t}\colon\mathfrak{g}\to\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}
. Differentiating the equation
\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}^{t}\circ\alpha_{t}|_{\mathfrak{i}}=0
yields
\frac{d}{dt}\big|_{t=0}{\pi^{t}_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}}(u)=\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ\frac{d}{dt}\big|_{t=0}{(-\alpha_{t})}(u)
for all
u\in\mathfrak{i}
.
The left-hand side is the deformation cocycle associated to the deformation of
\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}
, see Remark
3.3
, and the right-hand side is, up to the minus sign, the deformation cocycle associated to the deformation of
\mathfrak{i}
, see Proposition
4.2
.
Since
\Pi
sends
\phi\in\wedge^{1}\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}
to
-\phi\arrowvert_{\mathfrak{i}}\in\wedge^{0}\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}
, this completes the proof of the second statement.
∎

Remark 4.6
.

One could hope that the natural inclusion

i\colon C_{\wedge}^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})\hookrightarrow C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})

is a quasi-isomorphism. However this is not the case in general: Let
(\mathfrak{g},\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}=0)
be an abelian Lie algebra. Then any vector subspace of
\mathfrak{g}
is an ideal in
\mathfrak{g}
. Here
\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}=0
, so the cohomologies are identical to the complexes themselves and therefore cannot be isomorphic.

However, in degree
0
, the vector spaces
C^{0}_{\wedge}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
and
C^{0}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
are equal, so also the
0
-cohomologies of the two complexes are equal.

4.2.3.
Relation with the deformation cohomology of
\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}

Now the deformation complex of the ideal
\mathfrak{i}
of
\mathfrak{g}
is set in relation with the deformation complex
\left(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}),\delta_{\overline{\ad}}\right)
of the quotient Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}
.

Proposition 4.7
.

The cochain complex
\left(C^{\bullet}_{\wedge}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}),\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}\right)
fits into the following short exact sequence of cochain complexes:

(25)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})[1],-\delta_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}^{\overline{\ad}}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})[1],-\delta_{\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(C_{\wedge}^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}),\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}\right).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\wedge}}

The first map is simply the inclusion
\wedge^{\bullet}\mathfrak{i}^{\circ}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}\hookrightarrow\wedge^{\bullet}\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}
, via the canonical isomorphism
\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}^{*}\colon(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})^{*}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{i}^{\circ}\subseteq\mathfrak{g}^{*}
.

Proof.

The kernel complex of
\Pi_{\wedge}
is given at degree
k
by:

\displaystyle\Ker^{k+1}(\Pi_{\wedge})

\displaystyle=\left\{\phi\colon\wedge^{k+1}\mathfrak{g}\to\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}\ |\ \phi(x_{1},\dots,x_{i},\dots,x_{k+1})=0\ \text{if}\ x_{i}\in\mathfrak{i}\ \text{for some}\ i\in\{1,\dots,k+1\}\right\}

(26)

\displaystyle=\wedge^{k+1}\mathfrak{i}^{\circ}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}\simeq C^{k+1}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})

via the natural isomorphism

(27)

\wedge^{k+1}\mathfrak{i}^{\circ}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}\to C^{k+1}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}),\quad\phi\mapsto\left(\overline{\phi}\colon(\overline{x_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x_{k+1}})\mapsto\phi(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k+1})\right).

Via this isomorphism, the inclusion
\left(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}),\delta_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}^{\overline{\ad}}\right)\hookrightarrow\left(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}),\delta_{\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}}\right)
is a cochain map, as shown in the following computation.

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}^{\overline{\ad}}(\overline{\phi})(\overline{x_{1}},\dots,\overline{x_{k+1}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{k+1}(-1)^{i+1}\overline{\ad}_{\overline{x_{i}}}(\overline{\phi}(\overline{x_{1}},\dots,\widehat{\overline{x_{i}}},\dots,\overline{x_{k+1}}))

\displaystyle+\sum_{i<j}(-1)^{i+j}\overline{\phi}([\overline{x_{i}},\overline{x_{j}}],\overline{x_{1}},\dots,\widehat{\overline{x_{i}}},\dots,\widehat{\overline{x_{j}}},\dots,\overline{x_{k+1}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{k+1}(-1)^{i+1}(\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}^{*}\overline{\ad})_{x_{i}}(\phi(x_{1},\dots,\widehat{x_{i}},\dots,x_{k+1}))

\displaystyle+\sum_{i<j}(-1)^{i+j}\phi([x_{i},x_{j}],x_{1},\dots,\widehat{x_{i}},\dots,\widehat{x_{j}},\dots,x_{k+1})

\displaystyle=\delta_{\pi_{{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}}}(\phi)(x_{1},\dots,x_{k+1})

for
\phi\in\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{i}^{\circ}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}
and
x_{1},\ldots,x_{k+1}\in\mathfrak{g}
.
∎

4.2.4.
Relation with Nijenhuis and Richardson’s deformation cohomology of an ideal

Let here
C_{\mathfrak{i}}^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})
be the graded vector space defined at degree
k
by

C_{\mathfrak{i}}^{k}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})\coloneqq\left\{\phi\colon\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{g}\to\mathfrak{g}\ |\ \phi(x_{1},\dots,x_{i},\dots,x_{k})\in\mathfrak{i}\ \text{if}\ x_{i}\in\mathfrak{i}\ \text{for some}\ i\in\{1,\dots,k\}\right\},

and equip it with the usual differential
\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}^{\rm ad}
, which preserves
C_{\mathfrak{i}}^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})
.

The obtained complex
C_{\mathfrak{i}}^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})
appeared in
[
34
]
as the complex controlling deformations of the Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{g},\mu_{\mathfrak{g}})
, such that
\mathfrak{i}
remains an ideal in it.

Recall the differential graded Lie algebra structure on
C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})[1]
defined in Section
2.3.1
by the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
.

Proposition 4.8
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be a Lie algebra and
\mathfrak{i}
be an ideal in
\mathfrak{g}
. The graded vector space
C^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak{i}}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})[1]
is a differential graded Lie subalgebra of
C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})[1]
and the linear map

(28)

\overline{\pi_{*}}\colon C_{\mathfrak{i}}^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})[1]\to C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})[1],\qquad\phi\mapsto\overline{\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ\phi}

with
\overline{\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ\phi}\in(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})[1])_{k}
defined as in (
27
) by

\overline{\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ\phi}(\overline{x_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x_{k+1}})\coloneqq(\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ\phi)(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k+1}),

for all
\phi\in C^{k+1}_{\mathfrak{i}}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g})
,
x_{1},\ldots,x_{k+1}\in\mathfrak{g}
,
is a dgLa morphism
5
5

5

This map can be extended to the whole complex
C^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak{i}}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})[1]
, i.e. is not only defined on non-negative degrees. The space
(C^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak{i}}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})[1])_{-1}
is
C^{0}_{\mathfrak{i}}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})=\mathfrak{i}
. The map
\overline{\pi_{*}}
is simply zero on this space.
. Furthermore, the cokernel complex
\frac{C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})[1]}{C^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak{i}}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})[1]}
of the inclusion
C^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak{i}}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})[1]\hookrightarrow C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})[1]
is isomorphic to
C_{\wedge}^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
via the linear map

(29)

\overline{\Pi}\colon\overline{\phi}\mapsto(-1)^{k}(\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ\phi)|_{\wedge^{k-1}\mathfrak{g}\wedge\mathfrak{i}}.

Proof.

The fact that the differential
\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}^{\ad}
and the Gerstenhaber bracket
\llbracket\cdot,\cdot\rrbracket
both restrict to
C^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak{i}}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})[1]
is a direct observation of their formulas. Recalling the complex
\Ker^{\bullet}(\Pi_{\wedge})
from (
26
), for
k\geq 1
the map
\overline{\pi_{*}}\colon C_{\mathfrak{i}}^{k}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})\to C^{k}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
factors as

C_{\mathfrak{i}}^{k}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})\to\Ker^{k}(\Pi_{\wedge})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}C^{k}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}),\quad\phi\mapsto\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ\phi\mapsto\overline{\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ\phi}.

Since the canonical identification
\Ker^{k}(\Pi_{\wedge})=\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{i}^{\circ}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}C^{k}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
is a cochain map, it is enough to check that also the map
C_{\mathfrak{i}}^{k}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})\to\Ker^{k}(\Pi_{\wedge})
is a cochain map. Compute

\begin{split}\delta_{\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}}(\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ\phi)(x_{1},\dots,x_{k+1})&=\sum_{i=1}^{k+1}(-1)^{i+1}(\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}^{*}\overline{\ad})_{x_{i}}(\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ\phi(x_{1},\dots,\widehat{x_{i}},\dots,x_{k+1}))\\
&+\sum_{i<j}(-1)^{i+j}\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ\phi([x_{i},x_{j}],x_{1},\dots,\widehat{x_{i}},\dots,\widehat{x_{j}},\dots,x_{k+1})\\
&=\sum_{i=1}^{k+1}(-1)^{i+1}\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}([x_{i},\phi(x_{1},\dots,\widehat{x_{i}},\dots,x_{k+1})])\\
&+\sum_{i<j}(-1)^{i+j}\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ\phi([x_{i},x_{j}],x_{1},\dots,\widehat{x_{i}},\dots,\widehat{x_{j}},\dots,x_{k+1})\\
&=(\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ\delta^{\ad}_{\mathfrak{g}}(\phi))(x_{1},\dots,x_{k+1})\end{split}

for
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
\phi\in C^{k}_{\mathfrak{i}}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})
and
x_{1},\ldots,x_{k+1}\in\mathfrak{g}
.

Next check that
\overline{\pi_{*}}\colon C_{\mathfrak{i}}^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})[1]\to C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})[1]
respects as well the Lie brackets. Take
\phi\in(C_{\mathfrak{i}}^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})[1])_{k}
and
\psi\in(C_{\mathfrak{i}}^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})[1])_{l}
and compute

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\overline{\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ\phi},\overline{\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ\psi}\right\rrbracket(\overline{x_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x_{k+l+1}})

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle(-1)^{kl}\!\!\!\!\sum_{\tau\in S_{(l+1,k)}}\overline{\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ\phi}\left(\overline{\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ\psi}\left(\overline{x_{\tau(1)}},\dots,\overline{x_{\tau(l+1)}}\right),\overline{x_{\tau(l+2)}},\dots\overline{x_{\tau(k+l+1)}}\right)

\displaystyle-\sum_{\tau\in S_{(k+1,l)}}\overline{\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ\psi}\left(\overline{\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ\phi}\left(\overline{x_{\tau(1)}},\dots,\overline{x_{\tau(k+1)}}\right),\overline{x_{\tau(k+2)}},\dots\overline{x_{\tau(k+l+1)}}\right)

for
x_{1},\ldots,x_{k+l+1}\in\mathfrak{g}
.
By definition, this is

\displaystyle(-1)^{kl}\!\!\!\!\sum_{\tau\in S_{(l+1,k)}}\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ\phi\left(\psi\left(x_{\tau(1)},\dots,x_{\tau(l+1)}\right),x_{\tau(l+2)},\dots x_{\tau(k+l+1)}\right)

\displaystyle-\sum_{\tau\in S_{(k+1,l)}}\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ\psi\left(\phi\left(x_{\tau(1)},\dots,x_{\tau(k+1)}\right),x_{\tau(k+2)},\dots,x_{\tau(k+l+1)}\right)=\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ\llbracket\phi,\psi\rrbracket\left(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k+l+1}\right).

Finally check that the linear map

\overline{\Pi}\colon\bigg(\frac{C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})}{C_{\mathfrak{i}}^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})}[1],-\overline{\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}^{\ad}}\bigg)\to\left(C_{\wedge}^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}),\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}\right),\qquad\overline{\phi}\mapsto(-1)^{k}(\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ\phi)|_{\wedge^{k-1}\mathfrak{g}\wedge\mathfrak{i}}

for
k\geq 1
and
\phi\in C^{k}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})
, is an isomorphism of cochain complexes. Consider the composition of the map

(\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}})_{*}\colon\big(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})[1],-\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}^{\ad}\big)\to\big(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})[1],-\delta_{\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}}\big),\quad\phi\mapsto\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ\phi

with

\Pi_{\wedge}\colon(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})[1],-\delta_{\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}})\to\left(C_{\wedge}^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}),\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}\right)

which was defined by

C^{k+1}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})\ni\phi\mapsto(-1)^{k+1}\phi\arrowvert_{(\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{g})\wedge\mathfrak{i}}

for all
k\geq 0
. Both maps are epimorphisms. The first one by Remark
2.12
, and the second one by definition of its codomain, see Proposition
4.7
.
Since the kernel of this composition is
C^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak{i}}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})[1]\hookrightarrow C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})[1]
, the conclusion follows since each epimorphism is the cokernel of its kernel, see Remark
2.12
.
∎

Remark 4.9
.

The complex
C_{\mathfrak{i}}^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})
appeared in
[
34
]
as the complex which controls deformations of the Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{g},\mu_{\mathfrak{g}})
such that
\mathfrak{i}
remains an ideal. This remark explains how this deformation problem is related to the one studied in this paper, at the infinitesimal level. A smooth deformation
(\mathfrak{i}_{t})_{t\in I}
of an ideal
\mathfrak{i}\subset\mathfrak{g}
induces as follows a smooth deformation
(\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}^{t})_{t\in I}
of the Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{g},\mu_{\mathfrak{g}})
such that
\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}^{t}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{i})\subset\mathfrak{i}
for all
t\in I
– i.e. the Lie bracket on
\mathfrak{g}
is deformed such that
\mathfrak{i}
remains an ideal in the deformed Lie algebras. This smooth deformation is defined, after choosing a smooth family of linear isomorphisms
(\alpha_{t})_{t\in I}\in\GL(\mathfrak{g})
such that
\alpha_{0}=\id
and
\alpha_{t}(\mathfrak{i})=\mathfrak{i}_{t}
, at each time
t
, by conjugation:

\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}^{t}(x,y)\coloneqq\alpha_{t}^{-1}\circ\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}\left(\alpha_{t}(x),\alpha_{t}(y)\right),\qquad\forall\ x,y\in\mathfrak{g}.

Differentiating the above equation at
t=0
yields

\bigg(\frac{d}{dt}\bigg|_{t=0}\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}^{t}\bigg)(x,y)=-\bigg(\frac{d}{dt}\bigg|_{t=0}{\alpha_{t}}\bigg)\circ\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}(x,y)+\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}\bigg(\frac{d}{dt}\bigg|_{t=0}\alpha_{t}(x),y\bigg)+\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}\bigg(x,\frac{d}{dt}\bigg|_{t=0}{\alpha_{t}}(y)\bigg)

for all
x,y\in\mathfrak{g}
.
Assume that
y\coloneqq u\in\mathfrak{i}
and apply the canonical projection
\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}
to the above equation:

\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ\bigg(\frac{d}{dt}\bigg|_{t=0}\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}^{t}\bigg)(x,u)=-\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ\bigg(\frac{d}{dt}\bigg|_{t=0}\alpha_{t}\bigg)([x,u])+\cancel{\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\bigg[\frac{d}{dt}\bigg|_{t=0}\alpha_{t}(x),u\bigg]}+\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\bigg[x,\frac{d}{dt}\bigg|_{t=0}\alpha_{t}(u)\bigg].

The right-hand side of this equation vanishes since it is the cocycle condition for
\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ\frac{d}{dt}\big|_{t=0}\alpha_{t}\in C^{0}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
, see (
24
) in the proof of Proposition
4.2
. Therefore
\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ\big(\frac{d}{dt}\big|_{t=0}\mu^{t}_{\mathfrak{g}}\big)\big|_{\mathfrak{g}\wedge\mathfrak{i}}=0
, which shows that
\frac{d}{dt}\big|_{t=0}\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}^{t}
indeed is an element of
C_{\mathfrak{i}}^{2}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})
.

4.2.5.
Relation with the deformation cohomology of the ideal as a Lie subalgebra

Let
\mathfrak{i}
be an ideal in a Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
. Recall that the deformation cochain complex of
\mathfrak{i}
as a Lie subalgebra of
\mathfrak{g}
is

(C^{\bullet}_{\rm def}(\mathfrak{i}\subset\mathfrak{g}),\delta_{\mathfrak{i}}^{\rm Bott})=(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{i};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}),\delta_{\tr})
. Let
\iota_{\mathfrak{i}}\colon\mathfrak{i}\to\mathfrak{g}
be the inclusion.

Proposition 4.10
.

The natural restriction

\res_{\wedge\mathfrak{i}}\colon C^{\bullet}_{\wedge}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{i};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})[1],

(30)

C^{k}_{\wedge}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})\ni\phi\mapsto(-1)^{k+1}\phi|_{\wedge^{k+1}\mathfrak{i}},

for
k\geq 0

is a cochain map and

H^{0}(\res_{\wedge\mathfrak{i}})\colon H_{\wedge}^{0}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})\rightarrow H_{\defor}^{1}(\mathfrak{i}\subset\mathfrak{g})

is injective. Furthermore, there is a commutative diagram of short exact sequences:

(31)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{\wedge}^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Ker^{\bullet}(\iota_{\mathfrak{i}}^{*})[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{i};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})[1]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\wedge}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\res_{\wedge\mathfrak{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\mathfrak{i}}^{*}}

where
\Ker(\iota_{\mathfrak{i}}^{*})=\{\phi\colon\wedge^{\bullet}\mathfrak{g}\to\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}\ |\ \phi|_{\wedge^{\bullet}\mathfrak{i}}=0\}
, and the top left and left inclusions are just given by the canonical inclusion
\wedge^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})^{*}\simeq\wedge^{\bullet}\mathfrak{i}^{\circ}\hookrightarrow\wedge^{\bullet}\mathfrak{g}^{*}
.

Note that
C^{0}_{\wedge}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})=\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}=C^{1}(\mathfrak{i},\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
, so
\res_{\wedge\mathfrak{i}}
is minus the identity in degree
0
. However, because the cocycle conditions on
C^{0}_{\wedge}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
and
C^{1}(\mathfrak{i},\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
are very different,
H^{0}(\res_{\wedge\mathfrak{i}})
is in general not surjective and so not an isomorphism in cohomology.

Recall as well that in (
31
), not the full complexes
C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})[1]
, etc, are considered, but only their truncations to non-negative degrees, as the top-right term does not have negative degrees. The map
\Pi_{\wedge}
is
not
defined on
(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})[1])_{-1}=C^{0}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})=\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}
,
and also e.g.
(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})[1])_{-1}=C^{0}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})=\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}
is
not
a vector subspace of
\Ker^{0}(\iota_{\mathfrak{i}}^{*})
which is
\{0\}
by definition.

Proof of Proposition
4.10
.

Compute using Proposition
4.5

and
\res_{\wedge\mathfrak{i}}\circ\Pi=\iota_{\mathfrak{i}}^{*}

\res_{\wedge\mathfrak{i}}\circ\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}\circ\Pi=\res_{\wedge\mathfrak{i}}\circ\Pi\circ(-\delta_{\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}})=\iota_{\mathfrak{i}}^{*}\circ(-\delta_{\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}}).

On
\phi\in C^{k}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
and
u_{1},\ldots,u_{k+1}\in\mathfrak{i}
, this is

-\sum_{i<j}\phi\left([u_{i},u_{j}],u_{1},\ldots,\widehat{u_{i}},\ldots,\widehat{u_{j}},\ldots,u_{k+1}\right),

which equals

(-\delta_{\mathfrak{i}}^{\rm Bott}(\iota_{\mathfrak{i}}^{*}\phi))(u_{1},\ldots,u_{k+1}).

Hence

\res_{\wedge\mathfrak{i}}\circ\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}\circ\Pi=\iota_{\mathfrak{i}}^{*}\circ(-\delta_{\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}})=-\delta_{\mathfrak{i}}^{\rm Bott}\circ\iota_{\mathfrak{i}}^{*}=-\delta_{\mathfrak{i}}^{\rm Bott}\circ\res_{\wedge\mathfrak{i}}\circ\Pi

Since
\Pi
is surjective on its image
C^{\bullet}_{\wedge}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
, this shows that
\res_{\wedge\mathfrak{i}}\circ\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}=-\delta_{\mathfrak{i}}^{\Bott}\circ\res_{\wedge\mathfrak{i}}
on
C^{\bullet}_{\wedge}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
, i.e. that
\res_{\wedge\mathfrak{i}}
in (
30
) is a cochain map.

If
\phi\colon\mathfrak{i}\to\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}
is an infinitesimal deformation of
\mathfrak{i}\lhd\mathfrak{g}
, then
H^{0}(\res_{\wedge\mathfrak{i}})[\phi]=[-\phi]\in H^{1}_{\defor}(\mathfrak{i}\subset\mathfrak{g})
,
where
[\phi]=\phi
on the left-hand side is the cohomology class of
\phi
as a closed element of
C^{0}_{\wedge}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})=\wedge^{0}\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}
, and
[-\phi]
on the right-hand side is the cohomology class of
-\phi
as an element of
C^{1}(\mathfrak{i},\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
. Assume that the image
H^{1}(\mathfrak{i},\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})\ni[-\phi]=0
, then there exists
\overline{x}\in\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}
, i.e. the class in
\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}
of some
x\in\mathfrak{g}
, such that
-\phi=\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ[\cdot,x]\colon\mathfrak{i}\to\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}
. But this vanishes because
\mathfrak{i}
is an ideal in
\mathfrak{g}
. As a consequence,
\phi=0
and so
H^{0}(\res_{\wedge\mathfrak{i}})
is indeed injective.

Next note that

\Ker^{\bullet}(\iota_{\mathfrak{i}}^{*})[1]=\{\phi\colon\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{g}\to\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}\ |\ k\geq 1\text{ and }\phi|_{\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{i}}=0\}=\bigoplus_{p\geq 0}\left(\mathfrak{i}^{\circ}\wedge\bigwedge^{p}\mathfrak{g}^{*}\right)\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}

naturally contains
C^{\bullet\geq 1}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})\simeq\wedge^{\bullet\geq 1}\mathfrak{i}^{\circ}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}
.
The commutativity of (
31
) is easy to check: the right-hand square commutes since
\res_{\wedge\mathfrak{i}}\circ\Pi=\iota_{\mathfrak{i}}^{*}
and the left-hand square is just the fact that via the identification
\wedge^{\bullet\geq 1}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}\simeq\wedge^{\bullet\geq 1}\mathfrak{i}^{\circ}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}
, the inclusion of
\wedge^{\bullet\geq 1}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}
has image in
\Ker^{\bullet}(\iota_{\mathfrak{i}}^{*})
.
∎

4.2.6.
Bringing everything together

Consider the following diagram.

(32)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{\mathfrak{i}}^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})[1]}{C_{\mathfrak{i}}^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{\wedge}^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\pi_{*}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\Coker}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{-90.0}{$\scriptstyle\simeq$}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\wedge}}

The short exact sequence at the bottom of the diagram was already found in (
31
) and the map
\overline{\pi_{*}}
was defined in (
28
). The isomorphism on the right-hand side is given by the map defined in (
29
).
The map
(\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}})_{*}\colon C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g})\to C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
sends
\omega\in\wedge^{\bullet}\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}
to

\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ\omega\in\wedge^{\bullet}\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}
. It is easy to check that this is a cochain map

\left(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}),\delta^{\ad}_{\mathfrak{g}}\right)\to\left(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}),\delta^{\ad^{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}}_{\mathfrak{g}}\right).

The square on the left-hand side of (
32
) commutes by definition of
\overline{\pi_{*}}
and
(\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}})_{*}
, and the square on the right-hand side commutes as well by definition of the involved linear maps.

Proposition 4.11
.

The cohomology
H_{\wedge}^{k}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
fits into two long exact sequences, which are related by the following commutative diagram (starting at
k=1
).

(33)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{\mathfrak{i}}^{k}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{k}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{k}_{\Coker}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{k+1}_{\mathfrak{i}}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{k}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{k}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{\wedge}^{k-1}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{k+1}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i};\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{k}(\overline{\pi_{*}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{k}((\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}})_{*})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}^{k-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{-90.0}{$\scriptstyle\simeq$}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{k+1}(\overline{\pi_{*}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial^{k-1}_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}}

with the connecting homomorphisms
\partial^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak{g}}
and
\partial^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}
defined as usual (see e.g.
[
2
, I.§2]
).

The proof of Proposition
4.11
is just a straightforward application of the following lemma to the diagram in (
32
). The proof of the following lemma can be found, among other sources, in
[
47
, Proposition 1.3.4]
,

Lemma 4.12
.

Let

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A_{1}^{\bullet},d_{A,1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(B_{1}^{\bullet},d_{B,1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(C_{1}^{\bullet},d_{C,1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A_{2}^{\bullet},d_{A,2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(B_{2}^{\bullet},d_{B,2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(C_{2}^{\bullet},d_{C,2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{2}}

be a commutative diagram of cochain complexes, with the two horizontal sequences being exact.

Then the maps
[F_{A}]
,
[F_{B}]
,
[F_{C}]
defined in cohomology by
F_{A}
,
F_{B}
and
F_{C}
intertwine the long exact sequences in cohomology induced by the short exact sequences
(A_{i},d_{A,i})\hookrightarrow(B_{i},d_{B,i})\twoheadrightarrow(C_{i},d_{C,i})
for
i=1,2
. That is, the following diagram of long exact sequences in cohomology commutes, with
k\geq 0
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{k}(A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{k}(B_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{k}(C_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{k+1}(A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{k}(A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{k}(B_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{k}(C_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{k+1}(A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial_{1}^{k-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\phi_{1}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[F_{A}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\psi_{1}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[F_{B}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial_{1}^{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[F_{C}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[F_{A}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial^{k-1}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\phi_{2}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\psi_{2}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial^{k}_{2}}

5.
Deformations of a Lie ideal as Maurer-Cartan elements

While for investigating the infinitesimal deformation theory of ideals, no choice of complement is needed, for finding the Lie structure on the deformation complex
C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
it is crucial to fix a complement
\mathfrak{i}^{c}
of the ideal
\mathfrak{i}\lhd\mathfrak{g}
in
\mathfrak{g}
, or equivalently a splitting
\sigma\colon\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}\to\mathfrak{g}
of the short exact sequence

(34)

0\rightarrow\mathfrak{i}\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}\rightarrow 0

of vector spaces defined by the epimorphism
\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\colon\mathfrak{g}\to\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}
of Lie algebras.
That is,
\mathfrak{i}^{c}
, respectively
\sigma(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})\subseteq\mathfrak{g}
is canonically isomorphic to
\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}
as a vector space.
The projection
\mathfrak{g}\to\mathfrak{i}
,
x\mapsto x-\sigma(\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}(x))
associated to the choice of splitting
\sigma
is denoted by
\pr_{\sigma}\colon\mathfrak{g}\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{i}
. The inclusion
\mathfrak{i}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{g}
is denoted by
\iota_{\mathfrak{i}}
.

Consider small deformations of
\mathfrak{i}\lhd\mathfrak{g}
in the sense of the following definition.

Definition 5.1
.

Let
\mathfrak{i}\lhd{\mathfrak{g}}
be a
k
-dimensional ideal in a Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot\,,\cdot])
and let
\mathfrak{i}^{c}\subset\mathfrak{g}
be a vector space complement of
\mathfrak{i}
. A
k
-dimensional subspace
\mathfrak{i}^{\prime}\subset\mathfrak{g}
is called a
small deformation of
\mathfrak{i}\lhd\mathfrak{g}
if
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{i}^{\prime}\oplus\mathfrak{i}^{c}
and
\mathfrak{i}^{\prime}
is an ideal in
\mathfrak{g}
.

Recall that the choice of
{\mathfrak{i}^{c}}
defines a smooth chart of
\Gr_{k}(\mathfrak{g})
around
\mathfrak{i}
. Its elements are the
k
-dimensional subspaces of
\mathfrak{g}
that are complementary to
{\mathfrak{i}^{c}}
.
Therefore, small deformations of
\mathfrak{i}\lhd{\mathfrak{g}}
are the subspaces of the vector space
\mathfrak{g}
which lie in the chart domain
U_{\mathfrak{i}^{c}}\simeq\Hom(\mathfrak{i},\mathfrak{i}^{c})
around
\mathfrak{i}\in\Gr_{k}(\mathfrak{g})
and are, in addition, ideals of the Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot])
.

5.1.
The Voronov dataset associated to an ideal in a Lie algebra

This section begins with the construction of a dgL
[1]
a structure on
C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
associated to an ideal
\mathfrak{i}
in a Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
and a choice of splitting of (
34
), that will then be the dgL
[1]
a controlling the deformations of
\mathfrak{i}
in
\mathfrak{g}
.

Proposition 5.2
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be a Lie algebra and let
\mathfrak{i}
be an ideal in it. Choose a splitting
\sigma\colon\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}\to\mathfrak{g}
of the short exact sequence (
34
) of vector spaces. The following quadruple is then a Voronov-dataset:

(1)

The graded Lie algebra
\mathbf{g}\coloneqq\big(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})[1]\oplus C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{gl}(\mathfrak{g})),\llbracket\cdot,\cdot\rrbracket\big)
defined in Section
2.3.2
.

(2)

\mathfrak{a}\coloneqq C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
with the inclusion
\mathbf{I}\colon\mathfrak{a}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{g}
defined by

\mathbf{I}(\psi)\in C^{k}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{gl}(\mathfrak{g}))
for
\psi\in C^{k}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
,

\mathbf{I}(\psi)(x_{1},\dots,x_{k},y)\coloneqq\sigma\big(\psi(x_{1},\dots,x_{k},\pr_{\sigma}(y))\big)

for
x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}\in\mathfrak{g}
and
y\in\mathfrak{g}
.

(3)

\mathbf{P}\colon\mathbf{g}\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{a}
defined by

\mathbf{P}(\phi,\psi)(x_{1},\dots,x_{k},u)\coloneqq\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\big(\psi\big(x_{1},\dots,x_{k},\iota_{\mathfrak{i}}(u)\big)\big)

for
(\phi,\psi)\in C^{k+1}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})\oplus C^{k}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{gl}(\mathfrak{g}))
,
x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}\in\mathfrak{g}
and
u\in\mathfrak{i}
.

(4)

\Theta\coloneqq\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}+\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}
.

Note that in the proposition above, the map
\mathbf{I}
depends on the choice of
\sigma
, while the map
\mathbf{P}
does not.

Proof.

By Section
2.3.2
,
(\mathbf{g},\llbracket\cdot,\cdot\rrbracket)
is a graded Lie algebra and
\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}+\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}
is a Maurer-Cartan element in it.

First check that
\mathfrak{a}\subset\mathbf{g}
is an abelian subalgebra via the inclusion
\mathbf{I}
. If
\psi_{1}\in C^{k_{1}}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
and
\psi_{2}\in C^{k_{2}}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
, then for
x_{1},\ldots,x_{k_{1}+k_{2}},y\in\mathfrak{g}

\displaystyle\llbracket\mathbf{I}(\psi_{1}),\mathbf{I}(\psi_{2})\rrbracket(x_{1},\dots,x_{k_{1}+k_{2}},y)=\big((-1)^{k_{1}k_{2}}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathbf{I}(\psi_{1})\circ\mathbf{I}(\psi_{2})-\mathbf{I}(\psi_{2})\circ\mathbf{I}(\psi_{1})\big)(x_{1},\dots,x_{k_{1}+k_{2}},y).

It is enough to check that
(\mathbf{I}(\psi_{1})\circ\mathbf{I}(\psi_{2}))(x_{1},\dots,x_{k_{1}+k_{2}},y)
vanishes.
By definition, this is

(35)

\begin{split}&\sum_{\tau\in S_{(k_{2},k_{1})}}\hskip-5.69046pt(-1)^{\tau}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathbf{I}(\psi_{1})\big(x_{\tau(k_{2}+1)},\dots,x_{\tau(k_{2}+k_{1})},\mathbf{I}(\psi_{2})(x_{\tau(1)},\dots,x_{\tau(k_{2})},y)\big)\\
&=\sum_{\tau\in S_{(k_{2},k_{1})}}\hskip-5.69046pt(-1)^{\tau}\hskip 2.84544pt\sigma\left(\psi_{1}\bigg(x_{\tau(k_{2}+1)},\dots,x_{\tau(k_{1}+k_{2})},(\pr_{\sigma}\circ\sigma)\big(\psi_{2}\big(x_{\tau(1)},\dots,x_{\tau(k_{2})},\pr_{\sigma}(y)\big)\big)\bigg)\right),\end{split}

which vanishes because
\pr_{\sigma}\circ\sigma=0
.

Next check that
\Ker(\mathbf{P})
is a graded Lie subalgebra of
(\mathbf{g},\llbracket\cdot,\cdot\rrbracket)
. Observe that the kernel of the projection
\mathbf{P}:\mathbf{g}\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{a}
consists of the following graded vector spaces:

\Ker(\mathbf{P})=\underbrace{C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})[1]}_{=:A}\oplus\left(\underbrace{C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{i})}_{=:B}+\underbrace{C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{\circ}\otimes\mathfrak{g})}_{=:C}\right).

By (
7
), the first component
A
is a graded Lie (sub)algebra of
\mathbf{g}
, while (
2.3.2
) implies immediately that
\llbracket A,B\rrbracket\subset B
and
\llbracket A,C\rrbracket\subset C
.
Last, (
10
) implies that
\llbracket B,B\rrbracket\subset B
,
\llbracket C,C\rrbracket\subset C
and
\llbracket B,C\rrbracket\subset B
. The element
\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}
is clearly in
A

and
\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}\in B\subseteq\Ker(\mathbf{P})
since
\mathfrak{i}\subset\mathfrak{g}
is an ideal.
Hence
\Theta=\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}+\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}\in\Ker(\mathbf{P})\cap\MC(\mathbf{g})
.
∎

The following shows that the dgL
[1]
a structure on
C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\ \mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
induced by the above Voronov dataset is the one controlling the deformation problem of the ideal
\mathfrak{i}\lhd\mathfrak{g}
. That is, the unary bracket

m_{1}\colon C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\ \mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})\to C^{\bullet+1}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\ \mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})

defined by

m_{1}(\psi)=\mathbf{P}\llbracket\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}+\ad^{\mathfrak{g}},\mathbf{I}(\psi)\rrbracket
equals the differential
\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}
. The following computation confirms this. For
\psi\in\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes(\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}})
,
x_{1},\ldots,x_{k+1}\in\mathfrak{g}
and
u\in\mathfrak{i}

\displaystyle m_{1}(\psi)(x_{1},\dots,x_{k+1},u)=\mathbf{P}\llbracket\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}+\ad^{\mathfrak{g}},\mathbf{I}(\psi)\rrbracket(x_{1},\dots,x_{k+1},u)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\llbracket\mu_{\mathfrak{g}},\mathbf{I}(\psi)\rrbracket(x_{1},\dots,x_{k+1},u)+\mathbf{P}\llbracket\ad^{\mathfrak{g}},\mathbf{I}(\psi)\rrbracket(x_{1},\dots,x_{k+1},u).

The first term is

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\big(\llbracket\mu_{\mathfrak{g}},\mathbf{I}(\psi)\rrbracket(x_{1},\dots,x_{k+1},\iota_{\mathfrak{i}}(u))\big)

\displaystyle=-\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\left(\sum_{\tau\in S_{(2,k-1)}}\hskip-5.69046pt(-1)^{\tau}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathbf{I}(\psi)\big(\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}(x_{\tau(1)},x_{\tau(2)}),x_{\tau(3)},\dots,x_{\tau(k+1)},\iota_{\mathfrak{i}}(u)\big)\right)

\displaystyle=-\hskip-8.5359pt\sum_{\tau\in S_{(2,k-1)}}\hskip-5.69046pt(-1)^{\tau}\psi([x_{\tau(1)},x_{\tau(2)}],x_{\tau(3)},\dots,x_{\tau(k+1)},u)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i<j}^{k+1}(-1)^{i+j}\hskip 2.84544pt\psi([x_{i},x_{j}],x_{1},\dots,\widehat{x_{i}},\dots,\widehat{x_{j}},\dots,x_{k+1},u)

and the second term is

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\big(\llbracket\ad^{\mathfrak{g}},\mathbf{I}(\psi)\rrbracket(x_{1},\dots,x_{k+1},\iota_{\mathfrak{i}}(u))\big)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\left((-1)^{k}\hskip-5.69046pt\sum_{\tau\in S_{(k,1)}}\hskip-5.69046pt(-1)^{\tau}\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}_{x_{\tau(k+1)}}\left(\mathbf{I}(\psi)(x_{\tau(1)},\dots,x_{\tau(k)},\iota_{\mathfrak{i}}(u))\right)\right)

\displaystyle-\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\left(\sum_{\tau\in S_{(1,k)}}(-1)^{\tau}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathbf{I}(\psi)\left(x_{\tau(2)},\dots,x_{\tau(k+1)},\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}_{x_{\tau(1)}}(\iota_{\mathfrak{i}}(u))\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\sum_{\tau\in S_{(1,k)}}(-1)^{\tau}\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\bigg(\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}_{x_{\tau(1)}}\big(\sigma(\psi(x_{\tau(2)},\dots,x_{\tau(k+1)},u))\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle-\sum_{\tau\in S_{(1,k)}}(-1)^{\tau}\hskip 2.84544pt\psi\big(x_{\tau(2)},\dots,x_{\tau(k+1)},[x_{\tau(1)},u]\big)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{k+1}(-1)^{i+1}\ad^{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}_{x_{i}}(\psi(x_{1},\dots,\widehat{x_{i}},\dots,x_{k+1},u))+\sum_{i=1}^{k+1}(-1)^{i}\hskip 2.84544pt\psi(x_{1},\dots,\widehat{x_{i}},\dots,x_{k+1},[x_{i},u]).

Together, the two terms add therefore up to
\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}(\psi)(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k+1},u)
. Note along the way that the equality
m_{1}=\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}
shows that
m_{1}
does not depend on the choice of the splitting
\sigma
.

Next consider the higher brackets.
Choose
\psi\in C^{k}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
and
\phi\in C^{l}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
.
Compute for
k\geq 1

\begin{split}\llbracket\llbracket\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}+\ad^{\mathfrak{g}},\mathbf{I}(\psi)\rrbracket,\mathbf{I}(\phi)\rrbracket&=\llbracket\llbracket\mu_{\mathfrak{g}},\mathbf{I}(\psi)\rrbracket,\mathbf{I}(\phi)\rrbracket+\llbracket\llbracket\ad^{\mathfrak{g}},\mathbf{I}(\psi)\rrbracket,\mathbf{I}(\phi)\rrbracket\\
&=\llbracket-\mathbf{I}(\psi)\circ\mu_{\mathfrak{g}},\mathbf{I}(\phi)\rrbracket+\llbracket(-1)^{k}\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}\circ\mathbf{I}(\psi)-\mathbf{I}(\psi)\circ\ad^{\mathfrak{g}},\mathbf{I}(\phi)\rrbracket\end{split}

and for
k=0

\begin{split}\llbracket\llbracket\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}+\ad^{\mathfrak{g}},\mathbf{I}(\psi)\rrbracket,\mathbf{I}(\phi)\rrbracket&=\llbracket\llbracket\mu_{\mathfrak{g}},\mathbf{I}(\psi)\rrbracket,\mathbf{I}(\phi)\rrbracket+\llbracket\llbracket\ad^{\mathfrak{g}},\mathbf{I}(\psi)\rrbracket,\mathbf{I}(\phi)\rrbracket\\
&=\llbracket 0,\mathbf{I}(\phi)\rrbracket+\llbracket\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}\circ\mathbf{I}(\psi)-\mathbf{I}(\psi)\circ\ad^{\mathfrak{g}},\mathbf{I}(\phi)\rrbracket.\end{split}

In the case
k\geq 1
, the term
-\llbracket\mathbf{I}(\psi)\circ\mu_{\mathfrak{g}},\mathbf{I}(\phi)\rrbracket
equals

(36)

(-1)^{(k+1)l}(\mathbf{I}(\psi)\circ\mu_{\mathfrak{g}})\circ\mathbf{I}(\phi)-\mathbf{I}(\phi)\circ(\mathbf{I}(\psi)\circ\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}).

Since

\begin{split}\left(\mathbf{I}(\psi)\circ\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}\right)(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k+1})&=\sum_{\tau\in S_{(2,k-1)}}(-1)^{\tau}\,\mathbf{I}(\psi)(\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}(x_{\tau(1)},x_{\tau(2)}),x_{\tau(3)},\ldots,x_{\tau(k+1)})\\
&=\sigma\circ\left(\sum_{\tau\in S_{(2,k-1)}}(-1)^{\tau}\,\psi(\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}(x_{\tau(1)},x_{\tau(2)}),x_{\tau(3)},\ldots,x_{\tau(k+1)})\right)\circ\pr_{\sigma}\end{split}

for all
x_{1},\ldots,x_{k+1}
and since
\pr_{\sigma}\circ\sigma=0
, both terms in (
36
) vanish.

Next,

(37)

\begin{split}&\llbracket(-1)^{k}\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}\circ\mathbf{I}(\psi)-\mathbf{I}(\psi)\circ\ad^{\mathfrak{g}},\mathbf{I}(\phi)\rrbracket\\
&=(-1)^{k+(k+1)l}\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}\circ\cancel{\mathbf{I}(\psi)\circ\mathbf{I}(\phi)}-(-1)^{k}\mathbf{I}(\phi)\circ\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}\circ\mathbf{I}(\psi)\\
&\quad-(-1)^{(k+1)l}\mathbf{I}(\psi)\circ\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}\circ\mathbf{I}(\phi)+\cancel{\mathbf{I}(\phi)\circ\mathbf{I}(\psi)}\circ\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}.\end{split}

At this point it is already easy to see that
\pr_{\sigma}\circ\sigma=0
and so
\mathbf{I}(\psi)\circ\mathbf{I}(\phi)=0
for all
\psi,\phi\in C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
, see also (
35
), imply that
m_{r}=0
for all
r\geq 3
: the iterated brackets with
\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}
all vanish, and the iterated brackets with
\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}
consist of sums of terms, each with at least one composition of the type
\mathbf{I}(\psi)\circ\mathbf{I}(\phi)
.

The case
r=2
remains to be computed explicitly. Choose again
\psi\in C^{k}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
and
\phi\in C^{l}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
and compute

\begin{split}&\left(\mathbf{I}(\phi)\circ\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}\circ\mathbf{I}(\psi)\right)(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k+l+1},y)\\
&=\sum_{\tau\in S_{(k,1,l)}}(-1)^{\tau}\left(\mathbf{I}(\phi)(x_{\tau(k+2)},\ldots,x_{\tau(k+l+1)})\circ\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}_{x_{\tau(k+1)}}\circ\mathbf{I}(\psi)(x_{\tau(1)},\dots,x_{\tau(k)})\right)(y)\\
&=\sum_{\tau\in S_{(k,1,l)}}(-1)^{\tau}\sigma\left(\phi\left(x_{\tau(k+2)},\ldots,x_{\tau(k+l+1)},\pr_{\sigma}\left[x_{\tau(k+1)},\sigma(\psi(x_{\tau(1)},\ldots,x_{\tau(k)},\pr_{\sigma}(y)))\right]\right)\right)\\
\end{split}

for all
x_{1},\ldots,x_{k+1},y\in\mathfrak{g}
. Then for all
x_{1},\ldots,x_{k+l+1}\in\mathfrak{g}
and
u\in\mathfrak{i}
,

\begin{split}&\left(\mathbf{P}\left(\mathbf{I}(\phi)\circ\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}\circ\mathbf{I}(\psi)\right)\right)(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k+l+1},u)\\
&=\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\left(\sum_{\tau\in S_{(k,1,l)}}(-1)^{\tau}\sigma\left(\phi\left(x_{\tau(k+2)},\ldots,x_{\tau(k+l+1)},\pr_{\sigma}\left[x_{\tau(k+1)},\sigma(\psi(x_{\tau(1)},\ldots,x_{\tau(k)},u))\right]\right)\right)\right)\\
&=\sum_{\tau\in S_{(k,1,l)}}(-1)^{\tau}\phi\left(x_{\tau(k+2)},\ldots,x_{\tau(k+l+1)},\pr_{\sigma}\left[x_{\tau(k+1)},\sigma(\psi(x_{\tau(1)},\ldots,x_{\tau(k)},u))\right]\right).\end{split}

This shows that
m^{\sigma}_{2}(\psi,\phi)(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k+l+1},u)
equals

\begin{split}&-(-1)^{k}\sum_{\tau\in S_{(k,1,l)}}(-1)^{\tau}\phi\left(x_{\tau(k+2)},\ldots,x_{\tau(k+l+1)},\pr_{\sigma}\left[x_{\tau(k+1)},\sigma(\psi(x_{\tau(1)},\ldots,x_{\tau(k)},u))\right]\right)\\
&-(-1)^{(k+1)l}\sum_{\tau\in S_{(l,1,k)}}(-1)^{\tau}\psi\left(x_{\tau(l+2)},\ldots,x_{\tau(k+l+1)},\pr_{\sigma}\left[x_{\tau(l+1)},\sigma(\phi(x_{\tau(1)},\ldots,x_{\tau(l)},u))\right]\right)\\
\end{split}

for all
x_{1},\ldots,x_{k+l+1}\in\mathfrak{g}
and
u\in\mathfrak{i}

In particular if
k=l=0
, then for all
x\in\mathfrak{g}
and
u\in\mathfrak{i}

\begin{split}m_{2}^{\sigma}(\psi,\phi)(x,u)&=-\phi\left(\pr_{\sigma}[x,\sigma(\psi(u))]\right)-\psi\left(\pr_{\sigma}[x,\sigma(\phi(u))]\right).\end{split}

This shows that for
\phi\in C^{0}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
, the bracket

m^{\sigma}_{2}(\phi,\phi)
is defined by

(38)

m^{\sigma}_{2}(\phi,\phi)(x,u)=-2\phi(\pr_{\sigma}[x,\sigma(\phi(u))])

for all
x\in\mathfrak{g}
and all
u\in\mathfrak{i}
.

Theorem 5.3
.

Given a Lie ideal
\mathfrak{i}\lhd(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot])
together with a choice of splitting
\sigma\colon\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}\to\mathfrak{g}
of (
34
), there is a bijection between

(1)

MC-elements of the
L_{\infty}[1]
-algebra
(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}),\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}=m_{1},m_{2}^{\sigma})
, and

(2)

small deformations of the ideal
\mathfrak{i}\lhd\mathfrak{g}
,

given by the correspondence:

C^{0}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}})\ni\phi\mapsto\Graph(\sigma\circ\phi)\subseteq\mathfrak{g}.

Proof.

The subspace
\Graph(\sigma\circ\phi)=\{u+\sigma(\phi(u))|u\in\mathfrak{i}\}
is an ideal in
\mathfrak{g}
if and only if
[\mathfrak{g},\Graph(\sigma\circ\phi)]\subset\Graph(\sigma\circ\phi)
. Equivalently, this means that for every
x\in\mathfrak{g}
and
u\in\mathfrak{i}
:

\displaystyle[x,u]+[x,\sigma(\phi(u))]=[x,u+\sigma(\phi(u))]\in\Graph(\sigma\circ\phi).

This is equivalent to

(\sigma\circ\phi)\left([x,u]+\pr_{\sigma}[x,\sigma(\phi(u))]\right)=\sigma\left(\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}[x,\sigma(\phi(u))]\right)

for all
x\in\mathfrak{g}
and all
u\in\mathfrak{i}
.
Applying
\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}
to both sides of this equation yields the equivalent equation

\phi([x,u])+\phi(\pr_{\sigma}[x,\sigma(\phi(u))])=\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}([x,\sigma(\phi(u))])=\left[\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}(x),\phi(u)\right]

for all
x\in\mathfrak{g}
and all
u\in\mathfrak{i}
.

On the other hand, compute the Maurer-Cartan equation of
(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}),\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}=m_{1},m_{2}^{\sigma})
for
\phi\in C^{0}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}})
:

\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}(\phi)+\frac{1}{2}m^{\sigma}_{2}(\phi,\phi)=0.

On
x\in\mathfrak{g}
and
u\in\mathfrak{i}
, this reads

\left[\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}(x),\phi(u)\right]-\phi([x,u])-\phi(\pr_{\sigma}[x,\sigma(\phi(u))])=0

by the considerations above.
This concludes the proof.
∎

Observe that in the case of a Lie subalgebra
\mathfrak{h}\subset\mathfrak{g}
, the controlling
L_{\infty}[1]
-algebra becomes a dgL
[1]
a
if the chosen complement
\mathfrak{h}^{c}
is a Lie subalgebra itself (such a complement does not always exist), since then a straightforward computation shows that the trinary bracket
m_{3}
defined in (
20
) vanishes.

However, in the case of a Lie ideal
\mathfrak{i}\lhd\mathfrak{g}
, the choice of a splitting
\sigma
which is a morphism of Lie algebras does not simplify the problem. However, picking a complement which is itself a Lie ideal turns the deformation problem into a linear one since then the binary bracket
m_{2}
vanishes. Once again, a choice like this, is not always possible – except for example when
\mathfrak{g}
is semisimple.

Remark 5.4
.

Given a dgL
[1]
a
(\mathbf{V},[\cdot,\cdot],\mathbf{d})
, two MC-elements
x_{0},x_{1}\in V_{1}[-1]
are called
gauge-equivalent
if there exists a smooth path
(x_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}
of MC-elements interpolating between
x_{0}
and
x_{1}
, together with a smooth path
(y_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}
of degree
-1
elements of
\mathbf{V}
, such that the following equation holds:

\frac{d}{dt}x_{t}=\mathbf{d}(y_{t})+\frac{1}{2}[y_{t},x_{t}].

It is worth mentioning that here there are no degree
-1
elements in the dgL
[1]
a
C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{i}^{c})
. This means that the gauge equivalence of its Maurer–Cartan elements is trivial. Therefore, the bijection of Theorem
5.3
intertwines the trivial gauge equivalence of the MC-elements with the trivial equivalence relation on the small deformations of the Lie ideal
\mathfrak{i}\lhd\mathfrak{g}
.

Recall from the considerations above Theorem
5.3
that after a choice of splitting
\sigma\colon\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}\to\mathfrak{g}
of (
34
), the unary bracket
m_{1}=\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}
of the
L_{\infty}[1]
-algebra
(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}),\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}=m_{1},m_{2}^{\sigma})
defined by
\sigma
does not depend of
\sigma
. The following theorem compares the binary brackets given by two choices of splittings of (
34
).
Consider two choices of splittings
\sigma,\nu\colon\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}\to\mathfrak{g}
of (
34
). Then the difference
\sigma-\nu
is an element of
\operatorname{Hom}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i},\mathfrak{i})
. This space is a representation of
\mathfrak{g}
via

\ad^{\rm Hom}\colon\mathfrak{g}\times\operatorname{Hom}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i},\mathfrak{i})\to\operatorname{Hom}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i},\mathfrak{i}),

\ad^{\rm Hom}_{x}(\phi)=\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}_{x}\circ\phi-\phi\circ\ad^{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}_{x},

i.e.

\ad^{\rm Hom}_{x}(\phi)(\bar{y})=[x,\phi(\bar{y})]-\phi[\bar{x},\bar{y}]

for all
x,y\in\mathfrak{g}
.

An easy computation shows that the difference
\pr_{\sigma}-\pr_{\nu}
equals
(\nu-\sigma)\circ\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\in\operatorname{Hom}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{i})
.

Lemma 5.5
.

Given a Lie ideal
\mathfrak{i}\lhd(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot])
together with two choices of splittings
\sigma,\nu\colon\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}\to\mathfrak{g}
of (
34
),
the
L_{\infty}[1]
-algebras
(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}),\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}=m_{1},m_{2}^{\sigma})
and
(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}),\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}=m_{1},m_{2}^{\nu})

satisfy

(39)

(m_{2}^{\sigma}-m_{2}^{\nu})(\psi,\phi)=-(-1)^{k}\phi\circ\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}^{\ad^{\rm Hom}}(\sigma-\nu)\circ\psi-(-1)^{(k+1)l}\psi\circ\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}^{\ad^{\rm Hom}}(\sigma-\nu)\circ\phi

for all
\psi\in\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}
,
\phi\in\wedge^{l}\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}

– with the compositions defined as in the second part of Remark
2.14
.

Proof.

By (
37
)

(40)

\begin{split}\left(m_{2}^{\sigma}-m_{2}^{\nu}\right)(\psi,\phi)=&(-1)^{k}\mathbf{P}\left(\mathbf{I}_{\nu}(\phi)\circ\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}\circ\mathbf{I}_{\nu}(\psi)-\mathbf{I}_{\sigma}(\phi)\circ\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}\circ\mathbf{I}_{\sigma}(\psi)\right)\\
&\quad+(-1)^{(k+1)l}\mathbf{P}\left(\mathbf{I}_{\nu}(\psi)\circ\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}\circ\mathbf{I}_{\nu}(\phi)-\mathbf{I}_{\sigma}(\psi)\circ\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}\circ\mathbf{I}_{\sigma}(\phi)\right)\end{split}

The first term of this sum equals

\begin{split}&(-1)^{k}\mathbf{P}\left(\mathbf{I}_{\nu}(\phi)\circ\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}\circ\mathbf{I}_{\nu}(\psi)-\mathbf{I}_{\sigma}(\phi)\circ\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}\circ\mathbf{I}_{\sigma}(\psi)\right)\\
&=(-1)^{k}\mathbf{P}\left((\mathbf{I}_{\nu}(\phi)-\mathbf{I}_{\sigma}(\phi))\circ\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}\circ\mathbf{I}_{\nu}(\psi)-\mathbf{I}_{\sigma}(\phi)\circ\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}\circ(\mathbf{I}_{\sigma}(\psi)-\mathbf{I}_{\nu}(\psi))\right)\\
&=(-1)^{k}\left(\phi\circ(\pr_{\nu}-\pr_{\sigma})\circ\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}\circ\nu\circ\psi-\phi\circ\pr_{\sigma}\circ\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}\circ(\sigma-\nu)\circ\psi\right)\\
&=(-1)^{k}\left(\phi\circ(\sigma-\nu)\circ\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}\circ\nu\circ\psi-\phi\circ\pr_{\sigma}\circ\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}\circ(\sigma-\nu)\circ\psi\right).\end{split}

The map
\sigma-\nu
has image in
\mathfrak{i}
and
\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}
preserves
\mathfrak{i}
, so

\pr_{\sigma}\circ\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}\circ(\sigma-\nu)=\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}\circ(\sigma-\nu)
.
Furthermore,
\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}\circ\nu\circ\psi
equals
\ad^{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\circ\psi
by the definition of
\ad^{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\colon\mathfrak{g}\to\mathfrak{gl}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
.
This leads to

\begin{split}&(-1)^{k}\mathbf{P}\left(\mathbf{I}_{\nu}(\phi)\circ\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}\circ\mathbf{I}_{\nu}(\psi)-\mathbf{I}_{\sigma}(\phi)\circ\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}\circ\mathbf{I}_{\sigma}(\psi)\right)\\
&=(-1)^{k}\phi\circ\left((\sigma-\nu)\circ\ad^{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}-\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}\circ(\sigma-\nu)\right)\circ\psi\\
&=-(-1)^{k}\phi\circ\delta^{\ad^{\rm Hom}}_{\mathfrak{g}}(\sigma-\nu)\circ\psi.\end{split}

Similarly, the term of the second sum equals

\begin{split}&-(-1)^{(k+1)l}\psi\circ\delta^{\ad^{\rm Hom}}_{\mathfrak{g}}(\sigma-\nu)\circ\phi.\end{split}

This shows that (
40
) equals

m_{2}^{\sigma}(\psi,\phi)-m_{2}^{\nu}(\psi,\phi)=-(-1)^{k}\phi\circ\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}^{\ad^{\Hom}}(\sigma-\nu)\circ\psi-(-1)^{(k+1)l}\psi\circ\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}^{\ad^{\Hom}}(\sigma-\nu)\circ\phi.\qed

Theorem 5.6
.

Given a Lie ideal
\mathfrak{i}\lhd(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot])
together with two choices of splittings
\sigma,\nu\colon\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}\to\mathfrak{g}
of (
34
),
the
L_{\infty}[1]
-algebras
(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}),\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}=m_{1},m_{2}^{\sigma})
and
(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}),\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}=m_{1},m_{2}^{\nu})
are isomorphic via the maps

f_{1}=\id_{C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})}\colon S^{1}\left(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})\right)\to C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}),

\begin{split}f_{2}\colon S^{2}\left(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})\right)&\to C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}),\\
(\psi,\phi)&\mapsto-\psi\circ(\sigma-\nu)\circ\phi-(-1)^{|\psi||\phi|}\phi\circ(\sigma-\nu)\circ\psi\end{split}

and

\begin{split}f_{r}=0\colon S^{r}\left(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})\right)&\to C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}),\end{split}

for all
r\geq 3
.

Proof.

Using the last part of Remark
2.14
, compute for all
\psi\in\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}
,
\phi\in\wedge^{l}\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}

\begin{split}m_{1}\left(f_{2}(\psi,\phi)\right)=\,&\,\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}\left(-\psi\circ(\sigma-\nu)\circ\phi-(-1)^{kl}\phi\circ(\sigma-\nu)\circ\psi\right)\\
=\,&-\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}(\psi)\circ(\sigma-\nu)\circ\phi-(-1)^{k}\psi\circ\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}^{\ad^{\Hom}}(\sigma-\nu)\circ\phi-(-1)^{k}\psi\circ(\sigma-\nu)\circ\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}(\phi)\\
&\,-(-1)^{kl}\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}(\phi)\circ(\sigma-\nu)\circ\psi-(-1)^{kl}(-1)^{l}\phi\circ\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}^{\ad^{\Hom}}(\sigma-\nu)\circ\psi\\
&\,-(-1)^{kl}(-1)^{l}\phi\circ(\sigma-\nu)\circ\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}(\psi)\\
=\,&-(-1)^{k}\psi\circ\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}^{\ad^{\Hom}}(\sigma-\nu)\circ\phi-(-1)^{(k+1)l}\phi\circ\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}^{\ad^{\Hom}}(\sigma-\nu)\circ\psi\\
&\,-\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}(\psi)\circ(\sigma-\nu)\circ\phi-(-1)^{(k+1)l}\phi\circ(\sigma-\nu)\circ\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}(\psi)\\
&\,+(-1)^{k}\left(-\psi\circ(\sigma-\nu)\circ\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}(\phi)-(-1)^{k(l+1)}\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}(\phi)\circ(\sigma-\nu)\circ\psi\right)\\
\overset{\eqref{diff_m2s}}{=}&\,m_{2}^{\sigma}(\psi,\phi)-m_{2}^{\nu}(\psi,\phi)+f_{2}\left(\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}(\psi),\phi\right)+(-1)^{k}f_{2}\left(\psi,\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}(\phi)\right)\\
=\,&\,m_{2}^{\sigma}(\psi,\phi)-m_{2}^{\nu}(\psi,\phi)+f_{2}\left(m_{1}(\psi),\phi\right)+(-1)^{k}(-1)^{k(l+1)}f_{2}\left(m_{1}(\phi),\psi\right).\end{split}

In other words, since
m_{1}=m_{1}^{\nu}=m_{1}^{\sigma}
and
f_{1}
is the identity

\begin{split}m_{2}^{\nu}(f_{1}(\psi),f_{1}(\phi))+m_{1}^{\nu}\left(f_{2}(\psi,\phi)\right)=f_{1}(m_{2}^{\sigma}(\psi,\phi))+f_{2}\left(m_{1}^{\sigma}(\psi),\phi\right)+(-1)^{kl}f_{2}\left(m_{1}^{\sigma}(\phi),\psi\right).\end{split}

Since
m_{1}=m_{1}^{\nu}=m_{1}^{\sigma}
and
f_{1}
is the identity, there is nothing further to check and
(f_{1},f_{2},f_{r}=0\mid r\geq 3)
is an isomorphism of
L_{\infty}[1]
-algebras, see Definition
2.4
.
∎

5.2.
The controlling
L_{\infty}[1]
-algebra of simultaneous small deformations

In the situation of the previous section, using Theorem
2.11
,
there is a cubic
L_{\infty}[1]
-algebra structure
\{\widetilde{m_{i}}\}_{i=1}^{3}
on the direct sum

(41)

\mathbf{g}[1]\oplus\mathfrak{a}\coloneqq C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})[2]\oplus C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{gl}(\mathfrak{g}))[1]\oplus C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}),

where, for any
6
6

6

Here
|x|
stands for the degree of
x
as an element of
\mathbf{g}
.

x,y\in\mathbf{g}
and
a,a_{1},a_{2}\in\mathfrak{a}
, the brackets are defined as follows:

(42)

\begin{split}\widetilde{m_{1}}(x+a)&\coloneqq-\left\llbracket\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}+\ad^{\mathfrak{g}},x\right\rrbracket+\mathbf{P}(x)+m_{1}(a)\\
\widetilde{m_{2}}(x,y)&\coloneqq(-1)^{|x|}\llbracket x,y\rrbracket\\
\widetilde{m_{2}}(x,a)&\coloneqq\mathbf{P}\llbracket x,\mathbf{I}(a)\rrbracket\\
\widetilde{m_{2}}(a_{1},a_{2})&\coloneqq m_{2}(a_{1},a_{2})\\
\widetilde{m_{3}}(x,a_{1},a_{2})&\coloneqq\mathbf{P}\llbracket\llbracket x,\mathbf{I}(a_{1})\rrbracket,\mathbf{I}(a_{2})\rrbracket.\end{split}

Definition 5.7
.

Let
(\mathfrak{g},\mu_{\mathfrak{g}})
be a Lie algebra and
\mathfrak{i}
an ideal in this Lie algebra. Choose a splitting
\sigma\colon\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}\to\mathfrak{g}
of (
34
). A
simultaneous small deformation
of
(\mu_{\mathfrak{g}},\mathfrak{i})
is a pair
(\mu^{\prime}\in\wedge^{2}\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{i}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathfrak{g})
such that
\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}+\mu^{\prime}
is a Lie bracket on
\mathfrak{g}
and
\mathfrak{i}^{\prime}
a vector subspace of
\mathfrak{g}
such that
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{i}^{\prime}\oplus\sigma(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
and
\mathfrak{i}^{\prime}
is an ideal in
(\mathfrak{g},\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}+\mu^{\prime})
.

Theorem 5.8
.

Let
\mathfrak{i}
be an ideal in a Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{g},\mu_{\mathfrak{g}})
and choose splitting
\sigma\colon\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}\to\mathfrak{g}
of (
34
). Consider
\phi\in\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}
and
\mu^{\prime}\in\wedge^{2}\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}
. Define
\ad^{\prime}\in\wedge^{1}\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{gl}(\mathfrak{g})
by
\ad^{\prime}_{x}(y)\coloneqq\mu^{\prime}(x,y)
for all
x,y\in\mathfrak{g}
, and set
\Graph(\sigma\circ\phi)=:\mathfrak{i}^{\prime}
.

Then the pair
(\mu^{\prime},\mathfrak{i}^{\prime})
is a simultaneous small deformation of
(\mu_{\mathfrak{g}},\mathfrak{i})
if and only if
(\mu^{\prime},\ad^{\prime},\phi)
is a Maurer-Cartan element of the
L_{\infty}[1]
-algebra
\big(\mathbf{g}[1]\oplus\mathfrak{a},\widetilde{m_{1}},\widetilde{m_{2}},\widetilde{m_{3}}\big)
defined in (
41
) and (
42
).

Proof.

The sum
\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}+\mu^{\prime}
is a Lie bracket on
\mathfrak{g}
and
\mathfrak{i}^{\prime}\lhd(\mathfrak{g},\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}+\mu^{\prime})
is an ideal in the obtained Lie algebra if and only if

\displaystyle\llbracket\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}+\mu^{\prime},\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}+\mu^{\prime}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=0,\quad\text{ see \eqref{Lie_Brackets_mu}},

\displaystyle(\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}+\mu^{\prime})(x,u+(\sigma\circ\phi)(u))

\displaystyle\in\Graph(\sigma\circ\phi)\quad\text{ for all }x\in\mathfrak{g},u\in\mathfrak{i}.

The first condition is equivalent to

(43)

\llbracket\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}+\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}+\mu^{\prime}+\ad^{\prime},\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}+\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}+\mu^{\prime}+\ad^{\prime}\rrbracket=0

by Section
2.3.2
, since
\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}+\ad^{\prime}
is then the adjoint representation of
\mathfrak{g}
associated to the Lie bracket
\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}+\mu^{\prime}
.
The second condition is equivalent to the following equality for all
x\in\mathfrak{g}
and
u\in\mathfrak{i}
:

(44)

\begin{split}&\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}(\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}(x,(\sigma\circ\phi)(u)))+\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}(\mu^{\prime}(x,u))+\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}(\mu^{\prime}(x,(\sigma\circ\phi)(u)))\\
&=\phi(\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}(x,u))+\phi(\pr_{\sigma}(\mu^{\prime}(x,u)))+\phi(\pr_{\sigma}(\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}(x,(\sigma\circ\phi)(u))))+\phi(\pr_{\sigma}(\mu^{\prime}(x,(\sigma\circ\phi)(u)))).\end{split}

The Maurer-Cartan equation for

\mu^{\prime}+\ad^{\prime}+\phi\in C^{2}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})[2]\oplus C^{1}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{gl}(\mathfrak{g}))[1]\oplus C^{0}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})

is

(45)

\widetilde{m_{1}}\left(\mu^{\prime}+\ad^{\prime}+\phi\right)+\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{m_{2}}\left(\mu^{\prime}+\ad^{\prime}+\phi,\mu^{\prime}+\ad^{\prime}+\phi\right)+\frac{1}{6}\widetilde{m_{3}}\left(\mu^{\prime}+\ad^{\prime}+\phi,\mu^{\prime}+\ad^{\prime}+\phi,\mu^{\prime}+\ad^{\prime}+\phi\right)=0.

Compute

\begin{split}\widetilde{m_{1}}\left(\mu^{\prime}+\ad^{\prime}+\phi\right)&\,=-\left\llbracket\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}+\ad^{\mathfrak{g}},\mu^{\prime}+\ad^{\prime}\right\rrbracket+\mathbf{P}(\mu^{\prime}+\ad^{\prime})+m_{1}(\phi)\\
&\,=-\left\llbracket\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}+\ad^{\mathfrak{g}},\mu^{\prime}+\ad^{\prime}\right\rrbracket+\mathbf{P}(\ad^{\prime})+\mathbf{P}\left\llbracket\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}+\ad^{\mathfrak{g}},\mathbf{I}(\phi)\right\rrbracket\\
&\overset{\eqref{eq2_LALArep}}{=}-\left\llbracket\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}+\ad^{\mathfrak{g}},\mu^{\prime}+\ad^{\prime}\right\rrbracket+\mathbf{P}(\ad^{\prime})+\mathbf{P}\left\llbracket\ad^{\mathfrak{g}},\mathbf{I}(\phi)\right\rrbracket,\end{split}

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{m_{2}}\left(\mu^{\prime}+\ad^{\prime}+\phi,\mu^{\prime}+\ad^{\prime}+\phi\right)=-\frac{1}{2}\left\llbracket\mu^{\prime}+\ad^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}+\ad^{\prime}\right\rrbracket+\tilde{m}_{2}\left(\mu^{\prime}+\ad^{\prime},\mathbf{I}(\phi)\right)+\frac{1}{2}m_{2}(\phi,\phi)\\
&\,=-\frac{1}{2}\left\llbracket\mu^{\prime}+\ad^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}+\ad^{\prime}\right\rrbracket+\mathbf{P}\left\llbracket\mu^{\prime}+\ad^{\prime},\mathbf{I}(\phi)\right\rrbracket+\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{P}\left\llbracket\left\llbracket\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}+\ad^{\mathfrak{g}},\mathbf{I}(\phi)\right\rrbracket,\mathbf{I}(\phi)\right\rrbracket\\
&\overset{\eqref{eq2_LALArep}}{=}-\frac{1}{2}\left\llbracket\mu^{\prime}+\ad^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}+\ad^{\prime}\right\rrbracket+\mathbf{P}\left\llbracket\ad^{\prime},\mathbf{I}(\phi)\right\rrbracket+\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{P}\left\llbracket\left\llbracket\ad^{\mathfrak{g}},\mathbf{I}(\phi)\right\rrbracket,\mathbf{I}(\phi)\right\rrbracket\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\frac{1}{6}\widetilde{m_{3}}\left(\mu^{\prime}+\ad^{\prime}+\phi,\mu^{\prime}+\ad^{\prime}+\phi,\mu^{\prime}+\ad^{\prime}+\phi\right)&\,=\frac{1}{2}\tilde{m}_{3}(\mu^{\prime}+\ad^{\prime},\phi,\phi)=\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{P}\left\llbracket\left\llbracket\mu^{\prime}+\ad^{\prime},\mathbf{I}(\phi)\right\rrbracket,\mathbf{I}(\phi)\right\rrbracket\\
&\overset{\eqref{eq2_LALArep}}{=}\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{P}\left\llbracket\left\llbracket\ad^{\prime},\mathbf{I}(\phi)\right\rrbracket,\mathbf{I}(\phi)\right\rrbracket.\end{split}

Using
\left\llbracket\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}+\ad_{\mathfrak{g}},\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}+\ad_{\mathfrak{g}}\right\rrbracket=0
(see Section
2.3.2
) and
\left\llbracket\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}+\ad^{\mathfrak{g}},\mu^{\prime}+\ad^{\prime}\right\rrbracket=\left\llbracket\mu^{\prime}+\ad^{\prime},\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}+\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}\right\rrbracket
, the left-hand side of (
45
) now reads

\begin{split}&-\frac{1}{2}\left\llbracket\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}+\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}+\mu^{\prime}+\ad^{\prime},\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}+\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}+\mu^{\prime}+\ad^{\prime}\right\rrbracket\\
&+\mathbf{P}(\ad^{\prime})+\mathbf{P}\left\llbracket\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}+\ad^{\prime},\mathbf{I}(\phi)\right\rrbracket+\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{P}\left\llbracket\left\llbracket\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}+\ad^{\prime},\mathbf{I}(\phi)\right\rrbracket,\mathbf{I}(\phi)\right\rrbracket.\end{split}

The first term has degree
2
in
\mathbf{g}\coloneqq C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})[1]\oplus C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{gl}(\mathfrak{g}))
, and the remaining terms have degree
1
.
Hence (
45
) holds if and only if (
43
) holds true
and

\mathbf{P}(\ad^{\prime})+\mathbf{P}\left\llbracket\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}+\ad^{\prime},\mathbf{I}(\phi)\right\rrbracket+\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{P}\left\llbracket\left\llbracket\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}+\ad^{\prime},\mathbf{I}(\phi)\right\rrbracket,\mathbf{I}(\phi)\right\rrbracket=0.

A straightforward computation using (
10
) shows that the latter equation reads

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\left(\ad^{\prime}_{x}(u)\right)+\pi_{\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}}\left(\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}_{x}((\sigma\circ\phi)(u))+\ad^{\prime}_{x}((\sigma\circ\phi)(u))\right)-(\phi\circ\pr_{\sigma})\left(\ad^{\mathfrak{g}}_{x}(u)+\ad^{\prime}_{x}(u)\right)

\displaystyle-\phi\left(\pr_{\sigma}\left(\ad^{\sigma}_{x}((\sigma\circ\phi)(u))\right)\right)-\phi\left(\pr_{\sigma}\left(\ad^{\prime}_{x}((\sigma\circ\phi)(u))\right)\right)=0

on
x\in\mathfrak{g}
and
u\in\mathfrak{i}
. Since
\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}(x,u)
lies in
\mathfrak{i}
and by definition of
\ad^{\prime}
, this is (
44
).
∎

Remark 5.9
.

As pointed out by the referee, it is worth noting that if one drops the assumption in Proposition
5.2
that
\mathfrak{i}
be a Lie ideal, and assumes only that it is a vector subspace of the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
, then the quadruple
(\mathbf{g},\mathfrak{a},P,\Theta)
is in fact a curved Voronov dataset. In this more general setting, it still holds that
\phi\colon\mathfrak{i}\to\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}
is a Maurer–Cartan element if and only if
\Graph(\sigma\circ\phi)
is a Lie ideal, thereby generalizing Theorem
5.3
. In light of this observation, Theorem
5.8
may be viewed as a straightforward application of
[
10
, Theorem 3]
.

6.
Geometric applications: obstructions, rigidity and stability

This section focusses on applying the machinery built above to a study of the local geometry of the (moduli) space of Lie ideals in a given Lie algebra.

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be a Lie algebra and choose
k\in\{0,\ldots,\dim\mathfrak{g}\}
.
Denote by
\bm{I}_{k}(\mathfrak{g})
the subset of the Grassmannian
\Gr_{k}(\mathfrak{g})
consisting of the space of
k
-dimensional Lie ideals inside
(\mathfrak{g},\mu_{\mathfrak{g}})
.

6.1.
The Kuranishi map and obstructions to deformations of ideals

Recall that the deformation cohomology

H^{\bullet}_{\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})=:H^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{i}\lhd\mathfrak{g})

of an ideal
\mathfrak{i}
in a Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
was defined in Section
4.1
, and that the underlying complex
(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i},\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom})
fits in the dgL
[1]
a
(C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}),\delta_{\mathfrak{g}\rhd\mathfrak{i}}^{\Hom}=m_{1},m_{2})
of Theorem
5.3
– writing now simply
m_{2}
for the binary bracket
m_{2}^{\sigma}
defined by a splitting
\sigma\colon\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i}\to\mathfrak{g}
of (
34
). Note that in this section the choice
\sigma
is sometimes replaced by the (equivalent) choice of a complement
\mathfrak{i}^{c}
of
\mathfrak{i}
in
\mathfrak{g}
.

Definition 6.1
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be a Lie algebra and let
\mathfrak{i}\lhd{\mathfrak{g}}
be an ideal in
\mathfrak{g}
.
The
Kuranishi map

\Kur_{\mathfrak{i}\lhd\mathfrak{g}}\colon H^{0}(\mathfrak{i}\lhd\mathfrak{g})\to H^{1}(\mathfrak{i}\lhd\mathfrak{g})

associated to the Lie ideal
\mathfrak{i}\lhd{\mathfrak{g}}
is defined as follows:

\displaystyle[\eta]

\displaystyle\mapsto\frac{1}{2}[m_{2}(\eta,\eta)].

This is well-defined since
m_{1}=\delta^{\rm Hom}_{\mathfrak{i}\lhd\mathfrak{g}}\colon C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})\to C^{\bullet+1}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
and

m_{1}(m_{2}(\eta,\eta))=2m_{2}(m_{1}(\eta),\eta)

for
\eta\in C^{0}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{i}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{i})
.

The following proposition is standard in deformation theory, but its proof is repeated here for the convenience of the reader.

Proposition 6.2
.

If
\Kur_{\mathfrak{i}\lhd\mathfrak{g}}\neq 0
, then the deformation problem of
\mathfrak{i}\lhd\mathfrak{g}
is
obstructed
: there exists a cohomology class
